B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

患有 麻疹 相关脑部疾病的巴基斯坦儿童的父母通过 WhatsApp 寻求相互支持 - Gavi、疫苗 联盟

Pakistani parents of children with deadly measles-linked brain disease find mutual support over WhatsApp - Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance
新闻简报The news report highlights the efforts of Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, in supporting immunisation programs globally. It mentions that parents in Kilifi, Kenya, and northern India, including Rohingya refugee families in Jammu, are finding mutual support through WhatsApp. The report underscores Gavi's role in vaccine support, strengthening health systems, and shaping vaccine markets. It also notes that Gavi has helped immunise over 1.2 billion children since 2000, contributing to a significant reduction in child mortality in lower-income countries. Additionally, the report references Gavi's collaboration with UNICEF to secure lower prices for the RTS,S/AS01 malaria vaccine, saving US$50 million for immunisation programs. These initiatives reflect Gavi's commitment to global health and immunisation efforts, particularly in addressing diseases like measles and malaria.

时间
跳至主要内容 国家中心 资助指南 次要导航 全球疫苗免疫联盟的新战略时期 全球疫苗免疫联盟跨越道德热线 English Français 主要导航 关于我们 关于我们 全球疫苗免疫联盟 (Gavi),疫苗 联盟帮助世界上一半以上的儿童接种疫苗,预防世界上一些最致命的疾病。
地点与对象
肯尼亚基利菲;印度北部;查谟的罗兴亚难民家庭。
事件
患有 麻疹 相关脑部疾病的儿童的父母通过 WhatsApp 找到相互支持;Gavi、疫苗 联盟强调了 麻疹 疫苗接种 的努力和相关的健康举措。
规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中未报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
Gavi、疫苗 联盟积极参与 疫苗 支持、加强卫生系统和塑造 疫苗 市场。该组织还致力于免疫工作,并已确保较低的疟疾 疫苗 价格以支持儿童免疫计划。

South Carolina’s measles outbreak jumped to Washington state 5 times in 2 weeks

South Carolina’s measles outbreak jumped to Washington state 5 times in 2 weeks
新闻简报A measles outbreak in South Carolina, which began on October 2, 2025, and was declared over on April 26, 2026, led to five distinct importation events into Washington state over two weeks in January 2026. These events were linked to domestic travel and unvaccinated individuals, resulting in 26 cases among residents. Whole-genome sequencing confirmed the virus's origin in South Carolina, and public health measures such as contact tracing, isolation, and quarantine were credited with preventing further spread. The outbreak underscores the growing risk of measles transmission within the US due to declining vaccination rates, as domestic travel now poses a greater threat than international incursions. Officials noted that only 18 of the country’s 4,588 confirmed measles cases this year were linked to international visitors, with 96 percent tied to domestic outbreaks. The study highlights the importance of timely genomic analysis in differentiating distinct importations from local transmission and informing outbreak response.
查看新闻五要素
时间
南卡罗来纳州的 暴发 于 2025 年 10 月 2 日开始,并于 2026 年 4 月 26 日宣布结束。2026 年 1 月,两周内发生了五起进口事件,第一次是 1 月 12 日,最后一次是 1 月 29 日。
地点与对象
暴发 主要影响南卡罗来纳州和华盛顿州。暴发 涉及南卡罗来纳州 997 例确诊病例和华盛顿州居民中的 26 例。
事件
华盛顿州卫生官员发现了五起来自南卡罗来纳州的 麻疹病毒 不同进口事件,导致该州出现了 麻疹病毒。这些事件与国内旅行和未接种疫苗的个人有关。
规模、影响与风险
南卡罗来纳州的 麻疹 是自 2000 年美国宣布消除 麻疹 以来规模最大的一次,确诊病例达 997 例。华盛顿州出现 26 例输入病例,由于采取了公共卫生措施,没有进一步传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
官员们利用全 基因组 测序和流行病学数据来追踪这五起输入事件。接触者追踪、隔离和检疫措施被认为有助于防止进一步传播。该研究强调了疫苗接种不足社区的国内旅行风险。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

South Carolina’s measles outbreak jumped to Washington state 5 times in 2 weeks

News Brief
A measles outbreak in South Carolina, which began on October 2, 2025, and was declared over on April 26, 2026, led to five distinct importation events into Washington state over two weeks in January 2026. These events were linked to domestic travel and unvaccinated individuals, resulting in 26 cases among residents. Whole-genome sequencing confirmed the virus's origin in South Carolina, and public health measures such as contact tracing, isolation, and quarantine were credited with preventing further spread. The outbreak underscores the growing risk of measles transmission within the US due to declining vaccination rates, as domestic travel now poses a greater threat than international incursions. Officials noted that only 18 of the country’s 4,588 confirmed measles cases this year were linked to international visitors, with 96 percent tied to domestic outbreaks. The study highlights the importance of timely genomic analysis in differentiating distinct importations from local transmission and informing outbreak response.
Original source text (English)
Since measles was declared eliminated in the US in 2000, the country’s occasional cases have come mainly from international travelers—visitors from far-flung places where the virus still spreads freely—or unvaccinated US residents who return from abroad with a highly infectious souvenir. But as vaccination rates have slipped nationwide in recent years and outbreaks grow bigger and more frequent, domestic travel now poses a greater risk than international incursions. In a study published this week in NEJM Evidence, Washington state health officials highlight that risk, detailing how an explosive outbreak in South Carolina reached their distant state through five distinct importation events over two weeks in January. In all, the introductions led to 26 cases among residents before officials stamped out the spread. South Carolina’s measles outbreak began on October 2, 2025, and was declared over on April 26, 2026. Officials confirmed 997 cases in the state during the outbreak. At the time, it was the largest measles outbreak recorded since the virus was declared eliminated, but Pennsylvania’s ongoing outbreak has since surpassed it. The study in Washington relied heavily on epidemiological data, linking cases among contacts and exposure sites. But also key to the work was whole-genome sequencing, which made clear that cases didn’t just stem from one single introduction of the virus from South Carolina that then spread among Washington residents; instead, it revealed five separate introductions. “As public health jurisdictions prepare to respond to increased measles importations linked to domestic measles outbreaks, while remaining vigilant for international importations, timely whole-genome sequencing can supplement epidemiologic information to help differentiate distinct importations from local transmission and inform outbreak response,” the authors conclude. Ars Video How The Callisto Protocol's Gameplay Was Perfected Months Before Release Importations In their analysis, health officials identified the first importation in a resident who fell ill with the telltale rash on January 12 after returning from a trip to South Carolina, where they were exposed to a person sick with measles. Genome sequencing found that the virus closely matched sequences from South Carolina cases. One close contact of the Washington case fell ill and was isolated. No other cases were linked to this importation. In a second importation event, South Carolina residents visited public places while infectious. As a result, four residents with close contact with the South Carolina visitors fell ill. While infectious, the four residents visited schools, healthcare facilities, and a church. Nine of their contacts and one person exposed in a public location fell ill, leading to a total cluster of 14 cases that spanned January 15 to February 17. In the third importation event, two state residents returned from South Carolina and fell ill on January 15 to 16. No additional cases were linked to these residents. Similarly, a fourth importation event occurred when a resident returned from travel. The person adhered to isolation and quarantine recommendations, and no cases were linked to this one. In the fifth importation event, a Washington state resident returned from out-of-state travel where they had contact with ill contacts and fell ill themselves on January 19. While contagious, they visited a school and a healthcare facility. Six close contacts fell ill. In all, this cluster of seven cases spanned January 19 to January 29. The genome sequences from five of the cases were nearly identical to those seen in South Carolina. Officials credit basic public health work of quarantines, isolation, and contact tracing with keeping the virus from spreading farther than it did. Contradicting Kennedy While the study highlights how readily this virus can spread among states amid declining vaccination, it also contradicts a common talking point from Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. Kennedy, a prominent anti-vaccine advocate, has downplayed the current measles surge in the US and aggressively defended against suggestions that his decades of anti-vaccine misinformation and disinformation have contributed to the surge in cases and drop in vaccination. Instead, Kennedy claims that there is a “global outbreak” and the US is “doing a better job than any country in the world at controlling the measles outbreak.” Both claims are false . Dozens of other countries have lower case rates than the US. While some countries, including neighboring Canada and Mexico, are also seeing large increases in cases, the soaring case tally in the US is not being driven by international imports, but by domestic spread in under-vaccinated communities. According to data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention , only 18 of the country’s 4,588 confirmed measles cases this year were reported among international visitors. Of all the cases, 4,404 (96 percent) are linked to a domestic outbreak. The US has had 43 new outbreaks this year. Beth Mole Senior Health Reporter Beth Mole Senior Health Reporter Beth is Ars Technica’s Senior Health Reporter. Beth has a Ph.D. in microbiology from the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill and attended the Science Communication program at the University of California, Santa Cruz. She specializes in covering infectious diseases, public health, and microbes. 58 Comments

Location and population
The outbreak primarily affected South Carolina and Washington state. The outbreak involved 997 confirmed cases in South Carolina and 26 cases among residents in Washington state.
Event
Washington state health officials identified five distinct importation events of the measles virus from South Carolina, which led to an outbreak in their state. The events were linked to domestic travel and unvaccinated individuals.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak in South Carolina was the largest since measles was declared eliminated in the US in 2000, with 997 confirmed cases. The importations led to 26 cases in Washington state, with no further spread due to public health measures.
Response, status and uncertainty
Officials used whole-genome sequencing and epidemiological data to trace the five importation events. Contact tracing, isolation, and quarantine measures were credited with preventing further spread. The study highlights the risk of domestic travel in under-vaccinated communities.

PA 麻疹病例达到1,360例;伯克斯县最新更新仍为7例 - 阅读鹰报

PA measles cases reach 1,360; Berks still at 7 in latest update - Reading Eagle
新闻简报截至 2026 年 10 月 27 日,宾夕法尼亚州已报告 44 个县确诊 1,360 例 麻疹 病例,其中伯克斯县有 7 例,兰开斯特县最多,有 478 例。麻疹 已导致 5 例与 麻疹 相关的死亡和 258 例住院治疗。麻疹 通过呼吸道飞沫传播,并在空气和表面上保持传染性长达两个小时。自 4 月底 暴发 开始以来,州卫生部门已在受影响社区注射了 6,700 多剂 MMR 疫苗接种,仅 9 月份就注射了超过 56,000 剂疫苗。官员建议在社区 传播 持续存在的县,为 6 至 11 个月大的婴儿额外提供早期剂量。富兰克林县一名 39 岁女性与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件仍在调查中。该部门继续监测 暴发 并敦促可能接触过的个人就医。
查看新闻五要素
时间
最新更新发布于2026年10月27日。暴发于4月下旬开始,10月23日以来报告的新增病例为22例。
地点与对象
暴发 正在宾夕法尼亚州 44 个县发生,其中特别提到伯克斯县有 7 例。兰开斯特县的病例数最多,为 478 例。其他受影响的县包括切斯特、黎巴嫩、斯库尔基尔、蒙哥马利和利哈伊。
事件
州卫生部报告了 44 个县确诊的 麻疹 暴发 病例,共有 1,360 例病例。暴发 包括与 麻疹 相关的死亡和某些地区正在进行的 传播。
规模、影响与风险
共有 1,360 例 麻疹 确诊病例、5 例与 麻疹 相关的死亡以及 258 例住院治疗。该病毒通过呼吸道飞沫传播,并在空气和表面上保持传染性长达两个小时。暴发 促使 疫苗接种 加大力度,包括临时诊所和额外剂量的建议。
应对、状态与不确定性
该州继续监测和报告 麻疹,并对与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件进行持续调查。疫苗接种 的努力正在扩大,该部门建议为受影响地区的婴儿增加剂量。暴发 仍处于活动状态,情况仍在审查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

PA measles cases reach 1,360; Berks still at 7 in latest update - Reading Eagle

News Brief
As of October 27, 2026, Pennsylvania has reported 1,360 confirmed measles cases across 44 counties, with Berks County at 7 cases and Lancaster County leading with 478. The outbreak has resulted in 5 measles-associated deaths and 258 hospitalizations. Measles spreads through respiratory droplets and remains infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours. The state health department has administered over 6,700 MMR vaccinations in affected communities since the outbreak began in late April, with more than 56,000 doses given in September alone. Officials recommend an additional early dose for infants between 6 and 11 months in counties with sustained community transmission. The measles-associated death of a 39-year-old woman in Franklin County is still under investigation. The department continues to monitor the outbreak and urges individuals who may have been exposed to seek medical attention.
Original source text (English)
An electron microscope image of a measles virus particle. (Cynthia S. Goldsmith; William Bellini, Ph.D./Centers for Disease Control and Prevention/TNS/TNS) By David Mowery | dmowery@readingeagle.com | Reading Eagle PUBLISHED: October 27, 2026 at 9:00 AM EDT Getting your Trinity Audio player ready... The statewide total of confirmed measles cases has reached 1,360 across 44 counties. Berks County remains at seven cases in the update issued Monday by the sate Department of Health. The state continues to report five measles-associated deaths. A department spokesperson said the measles-associated death of a 39-year-old woman announced by the Franklin County coroner on Oct. 12 is still under investigation by department experts. The department’s update noted the following: • New positive cases since Oct. 23: 22 • Total counties: 44 • Total cumulative cases: 1,360 • Total hospitalizations: 258 • Total confirmed measles-associated deaths: 5 • Measles cases among vaccinated individuals: 4 (less than 1% of cases) According to the department’s dashboard at pa.gov/measles , Lancaster County remains at the top in cases with 478. Cases in the region: Chester County, 93; Lebanon County, 21; Schuylkill County, 7; Montgomery County, 3; and Lehigh County, 2. State health center staff have administered almost 6,700 measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccinations at 228 pop-up vaccination clinics in affected communities since the outbreak began in late April, officials said. Overall, the department has given more than 10,300 MMR vaccine doses this year. Officials said healthcare providers continue to see an increase in residents interested in the MMR vaccinations. Providers typically administer about 25,000 doses of the vaccine a month. They said more than 56,000 doses were given in September and that more than 39,000 MMR vaccines were given in the first three weeks of October. Because measles continues to spread, the department said it recommends an additional early zero dose for infants between the ages of 6 and 11 months who live in or travel to counties flagged on the measles dashboard for sustained community transmission. They said the early dose does not replace the two routine doses. Children 12 months and older who have not received any dose should get one now and a second dose at least 28 days later. Those who have two documented doses of MMR do not need another, the department said. Adults who have only one dose or cannot find their vaccination records should talk with a healthcare provider or pharmacist about getting vaccinated. Measles is spread when someone who is infected breathes, coughs or sneezes. The virus remains infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours after the infected person leaves the area, the department said. Symptoms begin with fever, cough, runny nose, and red, watery eyes (conjunctivitis), followed by a rash that starts on the head and spreads downward. Without proper protection or supportive care, measles can be deadly, officials said. Symptoms can start seven to 21 days after exposure. If you believe you were exposed to measles and are experiencing symptoms, you are advised to contact your healthcare provider or call the health department’s hotline at 877-PA-HEALTH (877-724-3258). Location and population
The outbreak is occurring across 44 counties in Pennsylvania, with specific mention of Berks County having 7 cases. Lancaster County has the highest number of cases at 478. Other affected counties include Chester, Lebanon, Schuylkill, Montgomery, and Lehigh.
Event
The state Department of Health reported a confirmed measles outbreak with 1,360 cases across 44 counties. The outbreak includes measles-associated deaths and ongoing transmission in certain areas.
Scale, impact and risk
There are 1,360 confirmed measles cases, 5 measles-associated deaths, and 258 hospitalizations. The virus spreads through respiratory droplets and remains infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours. The outbreak has prompted increased vaccination efforts, including pop-up clinics and recommendations for additional doses.
Response, status and uncertainty
The state continues to monitor and report on the outbreak, with ongoing investigations into measles-associated deaths. Vaccination efforts are being expanded, and the department recommends additional doses for infants in affected areas. The outbreak is still active, and the situation remains under review.

专家称,“煤矿里的金丝雀”:麻疹 的复苏暴露了美国公共卫生的弱点 - CNBC

A 'canary in the coal mine': Measles resurgence exposes weaknesses in U.S. public health, experts say - CNBC
新闻简报美国正在经历一次显著的麻疹疫情反弹,今年已报告超过4,400例病例和至少3例死亡,这是近三十年来最严重的暴发疫情。公共卫生专家警告称,这种疫情反弹凸显了疫苗接种接种率下降、削弱了人们对卫生机构的信任,并且在说服人们保护自己和社区方面面临挑战。麻疹、腮腺炎和风疹(MMR)疫苗在完成两剂接种后有效性为97%,但2025-2026学年中,幼儿园儿童的疫苗接种接种率降至92.4%,低于推荐的95%标准。这导致280,000名儿童未接种疫苗,且67%的县免疫接种率低于95%。疫情反弹被视为更广泛公共卫生脆弱性的‘煤窑里的金丝雀’,包括如果疫苗接种接种率不改善,麻疹可能再次成为地方性流行病的潜在风险。此外,CDC也面临追踪暴发的挑战,因为其对与麻疹相关的死亡报告存在延迟。专家认为,当前形势反映了公共卫生基础设施和联邦疫苗政策的更广泛问题,这可能会进一步削弱人们对疫苗接种的信心。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年10月27日,2025-2026学年,2026年11月中旬。
地点与对象
美国、幼儿园、疫苗接种 覆盖率较低的社区。
事件
据报道,麻疹 在美国卷土重来,今年已有 4,400 多例病例,至少三人死亡,这是该国三十多年来最严重的 麻疹。公共卫生专家强调了这种复苏的影响。
规模、影响与风险
今年全国报告了 4,400 多例 麻疹 病例,至少三人死亡。2025-2026 学年,疫苗接种 幼儿园儿童覆盖率下降至 92.4%,低于 95% 的门槛。280,000 名幼儿园儿童未完成 MMR 疫苗接种 系列。67%的县免疫接种率低于95%。这种病毒的复苏标志着公共卫生防御的脆弱性以及婴儿和免疫功能低下个体面临的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
在泛美卫生组织即将进行的审查中,该公司有望失去其官方 麻疹 消除状态。CDC 不再立即将国家报告的 麻疹 相关死亡纳入其全国统计,这使得实时跟踪变得困难。公共卫生基础设施因大流行的影响而被削弱,联邦政策的变化可能会进一步削弱 疫苗 的信心。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

A 'canary in the coal mine': Measles resurgence exposes weaknesses in U.S. public health, experts say - CNBC

News Brief
The U.S. is experiencing a significant measles resurgence, with over 4,400 cases and at least three deaths reported this year, marking the worst outbreak in more than three decades. Public health experts warn that this resurgence highlights declining vaccination rates, eroding trust in health institutions, and challenges in persuading people to protect themselves and their communities. The measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine is 97% effective after two doses, but vaccination coverage among kindergarteners dropped to 92.4% in the 2025-2026 school year, below the recommended 95% threshold. This has left 280,000 children unvaccinated and 67% of counties with immunization rates below 95%. The resurgence is seen as a 'canary in the coal mine' for broader public health vulnerabilities, including the potential for measles to become endemic again if vaccination rates do not improve. The CDC is also facing challenges in tracking outbreaks due to its delayed reporting of measles-associated deaths. Experts argue that the current situation reflects broader issues with public health infrastructure and federal vaccine policy, which may further undermine confidence in immunizations.
Original source text (English)
Key Points More than 4,400 measles cases and at least three deaths have been reported nationwide this year, marking the country’s worst outbreak in more than three decades. Several public health experts told CNBC the resurgence exposes declining vaccination rates and waning trust in health institutions as well as growing challenges in persuading people to protect themselves, their children and their communities. Those same vulnerabilities could complicate the nation’s response to future infectious disease threats, from other vaccine-preventable illnesses to emerging pathogens. watch now VIDEO 3:03 03:03 What the measles outbreak signals for the U.S. Health and Science For nearly two years, the U.S. has had to swallow an uncomfortable reality: A highly infectious disease it once eliminated is spreading again. More than 4,400 measles cases and at least three deaths have been reported nationwide this year, marking the country’s worst outbreak in more than three decades. The spike followed nearly 2,300 cases in 2025. But many public health experts argue that the return of measles isn’t the only concerning development — it’s also what the resurgence signals about America that’s causing alarm. Measles is often described as one of the most contagious viruses, and it is uniquely unforgiving of weaknesses in public health defenses. A safe and highly effective vaccine has existed for decades. But the virus’s spread exposes declining vaccination rates, eroding trust in health institutions and growing challenges in persuading people to protect themselves, their children and their communities, experts said. Together, those vulnerabilities have made measles a revealing test of the country’s public health defenses. For some experts, the resurgence of a disease the U.S. eliminated more than two decades ago is a warning sign of its ability to contain future threats, from other vaccine-preventable diseases to emerging pathogens. “Measles is the canary in the coal mine for so many different reasons,” said Jennifer Nuzzo, an epidemiology professor and director of the Pandemic Center at Brown University School of Public Health. “It’s the canary in the coal mine for other vaccine-preventable illnesses, where we could see a return of things that used to harm kids, but also for very acute, no-notice dangerous infectious disease emergencies.” The U.S. is on track to lose its official measles elimination status during an upcoming review by the Pan American Health Organization , which will meet in Washington, D.C., in mid-November. “It says a lot about a country if it is unable to control measles,” said Nuzzo, who is one of 10 experts CNBC spoke with about the ongoing outbreak and its implications. Pockets of vulnerability A measles advisory is shown tacked to a bulletin board outside Gaines County Courthouse in Seminole, Texas, April 9, 2025. Brandon Bell | Getty Images The U.S. has the tools to prevent measles from spreading. But pockets of low vaccination coverage are leaving the country vulnerable. The measles, mumps and rubella, or MMR, vaccine is about 97% effective against measles after two doses, while a single dose is 93% effective, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Yet data aggregated by the CDC shows that vaccination coverage for that shot among U.S. kindergarteners fell to 92.4% in the 2025-2026 school year, which is below the 95% threshold public health officials generally recommend to prevent sustained transmission. That translates to 280,000 kindergarteners who did not complete their MMR vaccination series, according to the CDC. Childhood vaccination exemptions in the U.S. also reached a record high 4.2% for incoming kindergartners during the 2025-2026 school year, up from 3.6% the previous year, the agency says. “The bottom line is that not enough people are vaccinating their kids,” said Brown’s Nuzzo. While some cases stem from international travel, much of the recent spread has been driven by local transmission in “too many” communities where vaccination coverage falls below 95%, she added. Among states across the U.S. collecting MMR vaccine data, 67% of counties and jurisdictions now have immunization rates below 95%, according to a September investigation by NBC News. Recent U.S. outbreaks have often occurred in “closely knit communities” where people have frequent contact and low vaccination coverage, said Dr. William Moss, an epidemiology professor at Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health. But declining vaccination rates do not mean most Americans have turned against immunization. Polling suggests broad support for childhood vaccines. A July KFF survey found that many Americans sit in a more uncertain middle ground about the shots, leaving room for conflicting information to influence their decisions. “This is not an edge-case issue,” Nuzzo said, adding that the vast majority of parents still vaccinate their children. Even if outbreaks are concentrated in a minority of communities, it can create a “dangerous situation” far from those areas, she said. Infants too young to be vaccinated and people who cannot receive immunizations for medical reasons can be particularly vulnerable when the virus spreads into communities with high vaccination rates. Those risks come as the Trump administration reshapes federal vaccine policy. President Donald Trump signed an executive order in August suggesting that the combined MMR vaccine be split into separate shots, while repeating false claims about its safety. Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. has also overhauled a key federal advisory committee responsible for recommending childhood immunizations, though the changes are frozen amid ongoing litigation. Several experts told CNBC that such moves risk further eroding confidence in vaccines and weakening efforts to protect children. Public health infrastructure was also “really decimated by Covid,” and the CDC in particular lost many experienced scientists and physicians “who had spent their lives working on vaccine-preventable diseases,” said Scott Weaver, a virologist who is the research director of Galveston National Laboratory. He added that the CDC, as of September, is also no longer immediately incorporating state-reported “measles-associated” deaths into its national published tally, making it harder to track outbreaks in real time. The agency lists three measles-related deaths in 2026, but Pennsylvania has reported at least five deaths. “We have folks in federal health leadership right now that are throwing gasoline on the fire, and that is not helping the situation,” said Jess Steier, a public health scientist and founder and CEO of Unbiased Science, an organization that aims to make complex scientific concepts understandable. Consequences beyond current outbreaks A medical assistant draws a measles, mumps and rubella vaccination to be administered to a young person at the pediatric office of Dr. Gary M. Kramer in Coral Gables, Florida, August 13, 2026. Joe Raedle | Getty Images If the U.S. fails to materially improve vaccination rates, the measles resurgence could extend beyond ongoing outbreaks. At “current” state-level vaccination rates, measles could become endemic again in the U.S., resulting in an estimated 851,300 cases over 25 years, according to a large-scale epidemiological model published in JAMA in April 2025 by researchers at Stanford and other institutions. Under that scenario, the model predicts the measles cases could lead to 170,200 hospitalizations and 2,550 deaths over that period. The model, which simulated infectious disease spread in the U.S. at various childhood vaccination levels, relies on immunization data from 2004 to 2023 for that baseline scenario. The model assumes that diseases are introduced by someone who travels abroad and brings them back, most commonly a U.S. citizen who isn’t vaccinated. While many people are currently immune through vaccination, outbreaks can increase in size and frequency if immunization rates decline over a longer period. Michael Osterholm, an epidemiologist and the director of the Center for Infectious Disease Research and Policy at the University of Minnesota, said he views the measles situation in Canada as a direct warning for the U.S. Canada lost its official measles elimination status in November after a multi-province outbreak that generated more than 5,400 cases across 10 jurisdictions in 2025. Canada has a population of more than 40 million people. “We have a population of 350 million. If you extrapolate, if we had the very same experience that Canada had over the next year or two, that would be up to 50,000 cases of measles in the U.S.,” Osterholm said. “I think that’s a potential future we have to be mindful of.” Other vaccine-preventable diseases An even larger measles outbreak isn’t the only potential problem. Vaccination coverage for other childhood diseases has similarly declined. The share of U.S. kindergartners vaccinated against polio fell to 92.4% in the 2025-2026 school year, while coverage for diphtheria, tetanus and acellular pertussis, or DTaP, dropped to 92%, according to CDC data . The U.S. has already seen a sharp rise in pertussis, or whooping cough. U.S. reported cases reached roughly 43,300 in 2024 and nearly 28,000 in 2025, up substantially from pandemic-era levels, according to the CDC. Brown’s Nuzzo said the resurgence reflects, in part, fewer people getting vaccinated and the fact that protection from the pertussis vaccine wanes over time. The simulation model published in JAMA also examined how falling childhood immunization rates could affect the future spread of several diseases previously eliminated from the U.S. through vaccines. Under current vaccination levels, the researchers found that diseases including rubella and polio were unlikely to become endemic in the U.S. But in a hypothetical scenario in which childhood vaccination rates fell by 50%, the model projected 9.9 million cases of rubella, 4.3 million cases of polio and 197 cases of diphtheria over 25 years. Rubella can cause devastating complications when an infection during pregnancy passes to a developing fetus, while polio can cause paralysis and death, said Dr. Nathan Lo, assistant professor of infectious diseases at Stanford Medicine. The projections are not predictions that these outbreaks will occur, but they illustrate how much risks could grow if vaccination coverage deteriorates. “What vaccines often buy you is not just the protection, but also less uncertainty,” said Mathew Kiang, an assistant professor of epidemiology and population health at Stanford Medicine who also worked on the simulation model. “I think even more frightening is just the really wide uncertainty that we just wouldn’t be sure of what could happen.” Preparing for the next threat The measles resurgence is also raising questions about how effective the U.S. can be at detecting and responding to emerging public health threats before they spread. “Public health is all about prevention,” said Dr. Manisha Juthani, president of the Association of State and Territorial Health Officials and commissioner of the Connecticut Department of Public Health. She said the country must maintain its ability to detect threats, invest in surveillance, and recognize that infectious diseases do not respect national borders. Those capabilities are crucial for curbing emerging pathogens, especially when scientists initially know little about how a new disease spreads, how severe it is or how to treat it. A pathogen could spread before vaccines or treatments are available, making early detection, clear public communication and coordination especially important. Other experts argue that weaknesses in those capabilities could also have implications for the country’s defenses against deliberate biological attacks. In an April 2025 op-ed , Steph Batalis, a research fellow at Georgetown University’s Center for Security and Emerging Technology, argued that the measles outbreak exposed a broader vulnerability in the U.S.′ biodefense strategy and, specifically, a weakened ability to deter potential biological attacks. She argued that the country’s struggle to contain a known, vaccine-preventable disease “might only embolden those who see our public health vulnerabilities as an opportunity.” But Osterholm, who works extensively on bioterrorism, said low MMR immunization rates and rising measles cases do not necessarily indicate that Americans would refuse a vaccine or antibiotic during a biological emergency. The shots and drugs used to counter biological threats can differ from those routinely administered to the public, he said, and the prospect of a severe outbreak could prompt urgency among the public to seek protection. That point highlights an important limitation: The measles resurgence by itself does not establish whether the U.S. could withstand entirely different or novel public health threats. But as a disease the U.S. knows how to prevent spreads, experts say the stakes of addressing those vulnerabilities extend to whatever might come next. is on track to lose its official measles elimination status during an upcoming review by the Pan American Health Organization. 美联社关于宾夕法尼亚州阿米什地区 麻疹 的报道的要点

Takeaways from AP's reporting on measles in Pennsylvania's Amish country
新闻简报文章报道了宾夕法尼亚州阿米什地区的一场麻疹 麻疹 麻疹,该地区已成为全美35年来最严重的麻疹 暴发的震中。暴发 与阿米什人和保守的门诺派教徒中的低 疫苗接种 率有关,由于文化和习惯原因而不是宗教教义,他们不太可能接种疫苗。宾夕法尼亚州今年已记录了超过 1,350 起 麻疹 案件,其中仅兰开斯特县就有超过三分之一。暴发 导致社区某些部分对 疫苗 的接受度有所提高,但对 疫苗 的怀疑仍然存在。公职人员正在宣传 麻疹 疫苗 的安全性和有效性,以鼓励 疫苗接种,因为该病毒很容易通过呼吸道飞沫和受污染的表面传播。美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 强调,美国各地 疫苗接种 发病率下降会增加 麻疹 暴发 的风险,而且这种情况正在影响整个社区。

时间
暴发 目前正在进行中,该文章于 2026 年 10 月 30 日发表。据报道,宾夕法尼亚州阿米什地区的 麻疹 是该国 35 年来最糟糕的情况,继 2025 年德克萨斯州和其他地方的门诺派人口中发生 暴发 后,麻疹 也随之而来。

宾夕法尼亚州的阿米什地区发生了 麻疹 麻疹 麻疹,该地区现在是全国 35 年来最严重的 麻疹 暴发 的震中。暴发 归因于阿米什人和门诺派中 疫苗接种 的比率较低,这导致社区某些部分对 疫苗 的接受度增加。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Takeaways from AP's reporting on measles in Pennsylvania's Amish country

News Brief
The article reports on a measles outbreak in Pennsylvania's Amish country, which has become the epicenter of the nation's worst measles outbreak in 35 years. The outbreak is linked to low vaccination rates among the Amish and conservative Mennonites, who are less likely to be vaccinated due to cultural and habitual reasons rather than religious doctrine. Pennsylvania has recorded over 1,350 measles cases this year, with more than a third in Lancaster County alone. The outbreak has led to increased vaccine acceptance in some segments of the community, but vaccine skepticism persists. Public officials are promoting the safety and effectiveness of the measles vaccine to encourage vaccination, as the virus spreads easily through respiratory droplets and contaminated surfaces. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) highlights that declining vaccination rates across the U.S. increase the risk of measles outbreaks, and the situation is impacting the entire community.
Original source text (English)
A man from the Plain community harvests crops in Intercourse, Pa., on Thursday, Oct. 8, 2026. Credit: AP/Jessie Wardarski By The Associated Press October 30, 2026 6:02 am EPHRATA, Pa. — Lancaster County, home to the largest Amish population in the U.S., is now the epicenter of the nation’s worst measles outbreak in 35 years. Vaccination rates have long been low among the Amish, and are now falling in the general population, creating the perfect conditions for the contagious virus — officially considered “eliminated” from the U.S. since 2000 — to spread here and beyond. Pennsylvania has the most U.S. cases, with over 1,350 this year, more than a third in Lancaster County alone, according to state health figures. That’s likely a big undercount, community leaders say, since many people with measles never see a doctor. The state has recorded three measles-associated deaths in Lancaster and two in other counties. The outbreak has especially hit the “Plain communities” of Amish and conservative Mennonites — so named for the religious groups’ modest dress and limited use of technology. Vaccination rates, and attitudes about vaccines, range widely among them. But overall, they are less likely to be vaccinated than the general population, due more to culture and habit than doctrine. The outbreak follows ones in 2025 in Mennonite populations in Texas and elsewhere. Most states have recorded cases this year. And though the Plain People avoid participating in many areas of society, they're not isolated. They interact with the general public at farmers markets, doctors' offices and elsewhere. So their new openness to vaccines after bearing the brunt of the outbreak impacts the entire community. A measles, mumps and rubella virus vaccine at Union Community Care clinic in Lancaster, Pa., on Thursday, Oct. 8, 2026. Credit: AP/Jessie Wardarski Here are some takeaways from The Associated Press’ reporting: While some think all Plain people reject vaccines, views actually vary Attitudes toward vaccines in Plain communities have alternately warmed and cooled over the years — most recently cooling in reaction to strict COVID-19 public health measures, according to community leaders and scholars. The measles outbreak is prompting many to accept the shots while reinforcing vaccine skepticism and suspicion in others. Those who avoid vaccines usually aren’t driven by ideology, community leaders and experts say. “There’s nothing in Amish theology or historic documents that you can find that specifically addresses or is opposed to immunization,” said Steve Nolt, director of the Young Center for Anabaptist and Pietist Studies at Elizabethtown College in Lancaster County. The main reason Plain people skip vaccinations isn't that they're against them, added Allen Hoover, an Old Order Mennonite and former administrator of the Parochial Medical Center in the town of New Holland. “It was simply, ‘Well, we never did before, and we came through all right.’” Dropping vaccination rates across the US increase the threat of measles Measles spreads easily when people who have it breathe, cough or sneeze, or through contaminated surfaces. “Measles is really the lion king of infectious diseases when it comes to transmission,” said Michael Osterholm, who directs the University of Minnesota’s Center for Infectious Disease Research and Policy. “No one in the United States of America who is susceptible to the measles virus, either having not been vaccinated or not having previously had it, should feel safe from measles. No one.” The risk grows as vaccination rates decline — which has been happening across the nation. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, 92.4% of U.S. kindergarteners in 2025-26 received the required MMR shots. That’s down from 95.2% in 2019-20. “Though Plain communities are an easy and recognizable target, declining childhood vaccination rates in our county are not limited to the Amish,” wrote Berwood Yost, director of the Center for Opinion Research at Franklin & Marshall College in Lancaster, in a recent commentary. “Kindergarten vaccination rates have declined in every central Pennsylvania county since 2020.” In fact, Pennsylvania’s overall kindergarten MMR vaccination rate fell to 93.2% by last year, which is below the 95% rate that makes an outbreak extremely unlikely in a community. During the COVID-19 pandemic, area doctors saw more vaccine hesitancy among patients within and outside of Plain communities. “The COVID vaccine just created a lot of distrust, a lot of conspiracy theories,” said Dr. Anne-Marie Derrico, chief medical officer at the nonprofit Union Community Care. “There’s still kind of an overall distrust of vaccines.” After a decline in trust, some minds are changing Lancaster County Commissioner Alice Yoder said public officials need to promote the message that the vaccine is safe and effective and encourage people to get it. That approach succeeded during Pennsylvania’s last measles outbreak in 1991. “There were lines of people in the Plain community that would come and get vaccinated,” she said. Dr. D. Holmes Morton, who founded the Central Pennsylvania Clinic in Mifflin County — which treats Plain patients with genetic diseases — said the same is happening now. “We are having more success at having vaccine days,” he said. Ultimately, this will keep everyone safer, said Dr. Diego Hijano of St. Jude Children’s Research Hospital. Getting the shot is “an act of love to your community,” he said. “You will get protected yourself. But in truth, you are actually protecting everyone around you.” ___ Associated Press religion coverage receives support through the AP’s collaboration with The Conversation US, with funding from Lilly Endowment Inc. 麻疹 暴发 暴露了持续免疫缺口的风险

Measles Outbreak Exposes Risks of Persistent Immunity Gaps
新闻简报加拿大的 麻疹 暴发 导致该国于 2025 年 11 月失去消除状态,该病毒集中在加拿大、美国、墨西哥等地区历来疫苗接种不足的社区。暴发 在 49 周内涉及 2,377 例(2,064 例确诊病例,313 例疑似病例),其中 90% 的病例发生在这些社区。麻疹 与 166 例住院、13 例入住 ICU、51 例妊娠期间感染、10 例先天性 麻疹 感染、1 例脑炎和 1 例婴儿死亡相关。暴发 强调了 麻疹 在存在免疫力差距的国家中卷土重来的风险,并强调了根据文化定制社区参与以建立对 疫苗接种 信任的重要性。研究人员指出,由于数据收集方面的挑战,包括不信任和对污名化的担忧,监测 数据可能不足以代表 暴发 的真实规模。由于疫苗接种不足的社区内部和之间的密切社会联系,暴发 还展示了跨境 传播 的潜力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
导致加拿大在 2025 年 11 月失去 麻疹 消除状态的 暴发 集中在加拿大、美国、墨西哥等地区历史上疫苗接种不足的社区,为跨境 传播 创造了机会。研究人员分析了麻疹期间(2024年10月28日至2025年10月6日)向安大略省公共卫生监测系统报告的麻疹病例。
地点与对象
暴发 集中在加拿大、美国、墨西哥等地区历史上疫苗接种不足的社区,而 跨物种溢出 进入更广泛人群的情况有限。在安大略省,暴发 对这些社区的影响尤为严重,这些社区的 疫苗 覆盖率历来较低且社交网络紧密。
事件
加拿大发生了 麻疹 麻疹,导致该国于 2025 年 11 月失去了 麻疹 消除状态。麻疹 涉及多个省份,在 49 周内与 2,377 例(确诊病例 2,064 例,疑似病例 313 例)相关。由于跨境 传播,暴发 与在其他国家检测到的独特 麻疹病毒 菌株有关。
规模、影响与风险
麻疹 导致 166 例入院、13 例入住 ICU、51 例妊娠期间感染、10 例先天性 麻疹 感染、1 例脑炎和 1 例婴儿死亡。大多数病例 (90%) 发生在历史上疫苗接种不足的社区成员中,只有有限的 跨物种溢出 进入更广泛的人群。麻疹 强调了 麻疹 在存在免疫力差距的国家中重新出现的持续风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员承认公共卫生 监测 数据的局限性,并指出病例数和相关并发症的数量可能比报告的要高。他们强调文化上适当的社区参与对于建立对 疫苗接种 的信任并防止未来出现 暴发 的重要性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles Outbreak Exposes Risks of Persistent Immunity Gaps

News Brief
A measles outbreak in Canada, which led to the country losing its elimination status in November 2025, was concentrated in historically under-vaccinated communities across Canada, the US, Mexico, and beyond. The outbreak involved 2,377 cases (2,064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks, with 90% of cases occurring in these communities. The outbreak was associated with 166 hospital admissions, 13 ICU admissions, 51 infections during pregnancy, 10 congenital measles infections, one case of encephalitis, and one infant death. The outbreak highlights the risk of measles resurgence in countries with immunity gaps and underscores the importance of culturally tailored community engagement to build trust in vaccination. Researchers note that surveillance data may underrepresent the true scale of the outbreak due to challenges in data collection, including mistrust and concerns about stigmatization. The outbreak also demonstrates the potential for cross-border transmission due to close social ties within and between under-vaccinated communities.
Original source text (English)
Mirage News Mirage News Mirage News Health 29 Oct 2026 8:58 pm AEDT Date Time BMJ 90% of cases occurred in historically under-vaccinated communities Findings offer insights for countries aiming to retain or regain their elimination status The outbreak that contributed to Canada losing its measles elimination status in November 2025 was concentrated in historically under-vaccinated communities with close social networks across Canada, the US, Mexico, and beyond, creating opportunities for cross-border transmission, concludes a study published by The BMJ today. The researchers say the findings highlight the ongoing risk of measles resurgence in countries that have previously successfully controlled the disease, and underscore the importance of culturally tailored community engagement to build trust and confidence in vaccination and prevent future outbreaks. Canada achieved measles-free status in 1998 after introducing a two-dose vaccine schedule and a national catch-up programme for school-aged children, but lost it in November 2025 after a measles outbreak that involved several provinces. In Ontario, the outbreak disproportionately affected historically under-vaccinated communities – groups with historically low vaccine coverage and close social networks across Canada, the US, and Mexico. To better understand the course of this outbreak and its impact, researchers analysed measles cases reported to Ontario's public health surveillance system during the outbreak period (28 October 2024 to 6 October 2025). The outbreak consisted of 2,377 cases (2,064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks. Most cases (90%) occurred among members of historically under‐vaccinated communities, with limited spillover into the broader population. Among the 2,377 cases, around three quarters (73%) occurred in individuals aged 19 years or younger, 2,121 (89%) were unvaccinated and 97 (4%) had unknown vaccination status. The outbreak was associated with 166 admissions to hospital, 13 admissions to an intensive care unit, 51 infections during pregnancy, 10 congenital measles infections, one case of encephalitis (brain inflammation), and one infant death. Laboratory tests identified a distinct measles virus strain that was subsequently detected elsewhere in Canada, and in the US, Mexico, and the Netherlands due to close social ties within and between under-vaccinated communities combined with the high transmissibility of measles. The researchers acknowledge that their analysis is subject to limitations inherent to public health surveillance data, and note that the number of cases and associated complications may be higher than what is reported in the study. In addition, the proportion of cases in under-vaccinated communities is likely to be conservative, reflecting challenges in data collection for a variety of reasons, including mistrust among some community members and concerns from public health staff about the risk of stigmatization of affected groups during the outbreak. However, they say this measles outbreak illustrates the fragility of measles elimination in the presence of substantial immunity gaps within domestically and globally interconnected communities. Limited spillover beyond these communities also highlights a high degree of vaccine acceptance in the population at large and the success of local partners in closing immunity gaps as part of the outbreak response, they add. "Strengthening of immunisation systems, improving vaccine coverage, and sustaining culturally appropriate engagement to continue to build trust in immunisation will be essential to prevent future vaccine preventable disease outbreaks and to support re-establishing measles elimination," they conclude. In a linked editorial, Jennifer Nuzzo at Brown University School of Public Health warns that Canada's loss of elimination status signals growing vulnerability to infectious disease threats, even in well-resourced high-income countries. She argues that elimination requires not just high vaccination coverage, but also sensitive disease surveillance, laboratory capacity, rapid outbreak investigations, timely reporting, effective public health responses, and public trust. Failing to stop measles within national borders also makes it harder for countries around the world to keep the disease at bay, she adds. She urges countries to recommit to measles elimination "not simply to restore a designation, but to stop a dangerous, preventable disease and strengthen the public health systems that protect their own populations and those of their neighbours from other serious infectious threats." /Public Release. Event
A measles outbreak occurred in Canada, leading to the country losing its measles elimination status in November 2025. The outbreak involved several provinces and was associated with 2,377 cases (2,064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks. B routine priorityPublic-health news

New measles cases in Macon County connected with previously reported infection, health officials say - CBS News

News Brief
Health officials in Macon County, Georgia, have confirmed three new measles cases linked to a previously reported infection. The cases were identified as close contacts of an earlier confirmed case in the county, which had contact with a measles case in Pennsylvania, where over 1,100 cases have been reported. All three new cases involved unvaccinated individuals, highlighting the risk of measles transmission among those not immunized. The Georgia Department of Public Health is actively notifying others who may have been exposed to the virus and urging them to check their vaccination status and monitor for symptoms. The outbreak underscores the importance of vaccination in preventing the spread of measles, a highly contagious viral disease that spreads through the air and can remain infectious on surfaces for up to two hours. Officials are working to contain the spread and ensure public safety.

Read Full Bio Dan Raby October 27, 2026 / 4:23 PM EDT / CBS Atlanta Add CBS News on Google Georgia health officials have confirmed three new cases of measles connected to a previous case earlier in October. The Georgia Department of Public Health said the three new cases confirmed in Macon County were close contacts of a previous case in the county confirmed on Oct. 12. The three new infected individuals were all unvaccinated. Investigators say they are working to notify others who may have been exposed to the virus. While details about the original case remain limited, officials say he or she had contact with a confirmed measles case in Pennsylvania, where the state reports an ongoing outbreak of the virus with more than 1,100 cases. Authorities believe the individual visited a LongHorn Steakhouse on Hampton Court in Perry on the night of Oct. 7 while they were infectious. The agency is urging anyone who was there during that window to check their vaccination status and watch for symptoms through Oct. 28. The new cases bring Georgia's total measles count this year to 25. Last year, the state reported 10 cases. Measles aka morbilli and rubeola is a highly contagious viral disease. It starts with a high fever, cough, and red eyes InnerVerse / Getty Images Possible signs of measles exposure and what you should do Measles first infects the respiratory tract, then spreads throughout the body, causing a high fever, runny nose, cough, red, watery eyes, and a rash. The rash generally appears three to five days after the first symptoms, beginning as flat red spots on the face and then spreading downward to the neck, trunk, arms, legs, and feet. When the rash appears, the fever may spike over 104 degrees Fahrenheit, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. The virus is highly contagious, spreads through the air, and can stay on surfaces for up to two hours after an infected person has been in a room. If you become sick or believe you have been exposed to measles, contact your healthcare provider immediately but do not go to the doctor's office without first letting them know about your symptoms. You can also call the Georgia Department of Public Health's Acute Disease Epidemiology Section at 404-657-2588 during business hours, Monday through Friday, or 1-866-782-4584 after hours on evenings and weekends. Officials say you should also stay at home and avoid contact with other people, especially anyone younger than 1 year old or those who have weakened immune systems. The Associated Press contributed to this report. 加拿大的 麻疹 暴发 暴露了消除疾病工作中的漏洞

Canada's measles outbreak exposes vulnerabilities in disease elimination efforts
新闻简报加拿大的 麻疹 麻疹 导致该国于 2025 年 11 月失去 麻疹 消除状态,暴露了疾病控制工作的漏洞。暴发 集中在加拿大、美国和墨西哥历来疫苗接种不足的社区,而 跨物种溢出 进入更广泛人群的情况有限。报告了超过 2,377 例病例,其中包括 2,121 例未接种疫苗的个体和 97 例 疫苗接种 状态未知的个体。麻疹 导致 166 例入院、13 例入住 ICU、51 例妊娠期间感染、10 例先天性 麻疹 感染、1 例脑炎和 1 例婴儿死亡。研究人员分析了安大略省公共卫生 监测 系统的数据,指出由于不信任和对污名化的担忧而导致数据收集的局限性。该研究强调了根据文化定制的社区参与和强大的 监测 对于防止未来 暴发 的重要性。麻疹 强调了在相互关联的社区中消除 麻疹 的脆弱性以及持续公共卫生努力的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
麻疹的报道时间为2024年10月28日至2025年10月6日,加拿大于2025年11月失去了麻疹的淘汰地位。该新闻报道的发布日期为2026年10月28日。
地点与对象
暴发 集中在加拿大、美国、墨西哥等地区历来疫苗接种不足的社区,其中安大略省的影响尤为严重。受影响人群包括 19 岁或以下的个人,其中 2,121 例未接种疫苗,97 例 疫苗接种 状态未知。
事件
加拿大发生了一起 麻疹 麻疹 事件,导致其失去 麻疹 消除资格。暴发涉及多个省份,并因社交关系密切而与跨境传播产生关联。《英国医学杂志》上发表的一项研究证实了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 导致 2,377 例(2,064 例确诊,313 例疑似)、166 例入院、13 例入住 ICU、51 例妊娠期间感染、10 例先天性 麻疹 感染、1 例脑炎和 1 例婴儿死亡。由于免疫缺口和跨境传播,麻疹死灰复燃的风险凸显。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员分析了暴发期间向安大略省公共卫生监测系统报告的病例。他们承认数据收集的局限性,包括社区成员之间的不信任和对污名化的担忧。暴发 应对措施包括努力缩小免疫差距和改善公共卫生系统。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Canada's measles outbreak exposes vulnerabilities in disease elimination efforts

News Brief
A measles outbreak in Canada, which led to the country losing its measles elimination status in November 2025, has exposed vulnerabilities in disease control efforts. The outbreak was concentrated in historically under-vaccinated communities across Canada, the US, and Mexico, with limited spillover into the broader population. Over 2,377 cases were reported, including 2,121 unvaccinated individuals and 97 with unknown vaccination status. The outbreak resulted in 166 hospital admissions, 13 ICU admissions, 51 infections during pregnancy, 10 congenital measles infections, one case of encephalitis, and one infant death. Researchers analyzed data from Ontario's public health surveillance system, noting limitations in data collection due to mistrust and concerns about stigmatization. The study highlights the importance of culturally tailored community engagement and robust surveillance to prevent future outbreaks. The outbreak underscores the fragility of measles elimination in interconnected communities and the need for sustained public health efforts.
Original source text (English)
The outbreak that contributed to Canada losing its measles elimination status in November 2025 was concentrated in historically under-vaccinated communities with close social networks across Canada, the US, Mexico, and beyond, creating opportunities for cross-border transmission, concludes a study published by The BMJ today. The researchers say the findings highlight the ongoing risk of measles resurgence in countries that have previously successfully controlled the disease, and underscore the importance of culturally tailored community engagement to build trust and confidence in vaccination and prevent future outbreaks. Canada achieved measles-free status in 1998 after introducing a two-dose vaccine schedule and a national catch-up programme for school-aged children, but lost it in November 2025 after a measles outbreak that involved several provinces. In Ontario, the outbreak disproportionately affected historically under-vaccinated communities - groups with historically low vaccine coverage and close social networks across Canada, the US, and Mexico. To better understand the course of this outbreak and its impact, researchers analysed measles cases reported to Ontario's public health surveillance system during the outbreak period (28 October 2024 to 6 October 2025). The outbreak consisted of 2,377 cases (2,064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks. Most cases (90%) occurred among members of historically under‐vaccinated communities, with limited spillover into the broader population. Among the 2,377 cases, around three quarters (73%) occurred in individuals aged 19 years or younger, 2,121 (89%) were unvaccinated and 97 (4%) had unknown vaccination status. The outbreak was associated with 166 admissions to hospital, 13 admissions to an intensive care unit, 51 infections during pregnancy, 10 congenital measles infections, one case of encephalitis (brain inflammation ), and one infant death. Laboratory tests identified a distinct measles virus strain that was subsequently detected elsewhere in Canada, and in the US, Mexico, and the Netherlands due to close social ties within and between under-vaccinated communities combined with the high transmissibility of measles. The researchers acknowledge that their analysis is subject to limitations inherent to public health surveillance data, and note that the number of cases and associated complications may be higher than what is reported in the study. In addition, the proportion of cases in under-vaccinated communities is likely to be conservative, reflecting challenges in data collection for a variety of reasons, including mistrust among some community members and concerns from public health staff about the risk of stigmatization of affected groups during the outbreak. However, they say this measles outbreak illustrates the fragility of measles elimination in the presence of substantial immunity gaps within domestically and globally interconnected communities. Limited spillover beyond these communities also highlights a high degree of vaccine acceptance in the population at large and the success of local partners in closing immunity gaps as part of the outbreak response, they add. "Strengthening of immunisation systems, improving vaccine coverage, and sustaining culturally appropriate engagement to continue to build trust in immunisation will be essential to prevent future vaccine preventable disease outbreaks and to support re-establishing measles elimination," they conclude. In a linked editorial, Jennifer Nuzzo at Brown University School of Public Health warns that Canada's loss of elimination status signals growing vulnerability to infectious disease threats, even in well-resourced high-income countries. She argues that elimination requires not just high vaccination coverage, but also sensitive disease surveillance, laboratory capacity, rapid outbreak investigations, timely reporting, effective public health responses, and public trust. Failing to stop measles within national borders also makes it harder for countries around the world to keep the disease at bay, she adds. She urges countries to recommit to measles elimination "not simply to restore a designation, but to stop a dangerous, preventable disease and strengthen the public health systems that protect their own populations and those of their neighbours from other serious infectious threats." Source: Journal reference: Wilson, S. E., et al . (2026). Impact of a large measles outbreak associated with transmission in the Americas and Canada’s loss of measles elimination status: Descriptive cohort study. BMJ. DOI: 10.1136/bmj-2026-101123. https://www.bmj.com/lookup/doi/10.1136/bmj-2026-101123

The affected population included individuals aged 19 years or younger, with 2,121 unvaccinated cases and 97 with unknown vaccination status.
Event
A measles outbreak occurred in Canada, leading to the loss of its measles elimination status. The outbreak involved several provinces and was associated with cross-border transmission due to close social networks. The event was confirmed by a study published in The BMJ.

The risk of measles resurgence was highlighted due to immunity gaps and cross-border transmission.

Measles Update: October 30, 2026 - Contagion Live
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 30 日,Contagion Live 报告 麻疹 PCR 检测有所增加,并指出国家实验室数据中的阳性率有所上升。文章强调了通过实验室检测对 麻疹 病例进行的持续监测,但没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露数量。据描述,该情况正在调查中,但没有进一步详细说明 暴发 的规模或风险级别。重点是检测方法和观察到的阳性率趋势,表明需要继续进行 监测 和分析。此更新是关于 麻疹 及其临床意义(包括 治疗 和住院注意事项)的更广泛讨论的一部分。文章没有提及任何硬排除或相关实体,证据以 麻疹病毒 为主要目标。

Measles Update: October 30, 2026 - Contagion Live

News Brief
On October 30, 2026, Contagion Live reported an increase in measles PCR testing, noting that positivity rates have risen in national lab data. The article highlights the ongoing monitoring of measles cases through laboratory testing, but it does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. The situation is described as under investigation, with no further details on the scale or risk level of the outbreak. The focus is on the testing methods and the observed trend in positivity rates, indicating a need for continued surveillance and analysis. This update is part of a broader discussion on measles and its clinical implications, including treatment and hospitalization considerations. The article does not mention any hard exclusions or related entities, and the evidence is based on the measles virus as the primary target.
